<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-055B29BD-B79C-4165-ABA0-871C50A3CE48'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Work out $\dfrac{5}{7}$ of $112$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {5} {7} \text { of } 112 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5} {7} \times 112 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 112} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 16 \times 7} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 16 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 16 \\\\\
&= 80
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$80$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {5} {7} \text { of } 112 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5} {7} \times 112 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 112} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 16 \times 7} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 16 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 16 \\\\\
&= 80
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$80$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-EEA9859C-584C-4844-B831-FFBE7DB5FC35'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Write down the next number in the sequence 

$15, \quad 21, \quad 27, \quad 33, \quad 39, \quad \boxed{\phantom{39}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding $6$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+6)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+6)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+6)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+6)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+6)}}  \\
15,  &&    21,  &&  27,  &&   33,  &&   39, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Missing number}   &= 39 + 6 \\\\
                         &= 45
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note

$

&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+6)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+6)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+6)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+6)}}  \\
15,  &&    21,  &&  27,  &&   33,  &&   39, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Missing number}   &= 39 + 6 \\\\
                         &= 45
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$45$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Using numerals write the number that is *six hundred and thirty less than one thousand*.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1000 - 630 \\\\
&= 370
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answer'>

$370$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-85EB3724-4385-40C7-99FC-BD96F06DD4F3'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Think of two integers that have a product of $18$ and a difference 
that is the same as one of the two integers that you are thinking of. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b            &= 18 \\\\
a - b                 &= b \\\\
a                     &= 2 \times b
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve them. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.

Substuting the value of $a$ from second equation to first equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b            &= 18 \\\\
2 \times b \times b   &= 18 \\\\
2 b^2                 &= 18 \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {18} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 9} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 9} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= 9 \\\\
b                     &= \sqrt {9} \\\\
b                     &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

Substuting the value of $b$ in second equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
a - b &= b \\\\
a - 3 &= 3 \\\\
a     &= 3 + 3 \\\\
a     &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

The two integers are $3$ and $6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b            &= 18 \\\\
a - b                 &= b \\\\
a                     &= 2 \times b
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve them. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.

Substuting the value of $a$ from second equation to first equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b            &= 18 \\\\
2 \times b \times b   &= 18 \\\\
2 b^2                 &= 18 \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {18} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 9} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 9} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= 9 \\\\
b                     &= \sqrt {9} \\\\
b                     &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

Substuting the value of $b$ in second equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
a - b &= b \\\\
a - 3 &= 3 \\\\
a     &= 3 + 3 \\\\
a     &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

The two integers are $3$ and $6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E79F071C-7363-457A-AB2E-0827A05F9BF1'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A recipe for making $12$ cupcakes includes the following ingredients:

Sugar $120 \ \text{g}$ 

Eggs $3$

Butter $150 \ \text{g}$ 

Flour $180 \ \text{g}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the quantities needed to make $16$ cupcakes.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

A recipe for making $16$ cupcakes includes the following ingredients:

Sugar
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {120} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 12} {12} \times 16 \\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {12} } {\cancel {12} } \times 16 \\\
&= 10 \times 16 \\\\
&= 160 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$ 

Eggs
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 4} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {3} } {\cancel {3} \times 4} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {4} } {\cancel {4} } \\\\
&= 4 
\end{aligned}
$ 

Butter
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {160} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 40} {3 \times 4} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 40} {3 \times \cancel {4} } \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {40 \times 16} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {640} {3} \\\\
&= 213.33 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$


Flour
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {180} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 \times 6} {6 \times 2} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel{6} } {\cancel{6} \times 2} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 \times 16} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {15 \times 2 \times 16} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {2}  \times 16} {\cancel {2} } \\\\
&= 240 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$Sugar 160 \ \text{g}, Eggs 4, Butter 213.33 \ \text{g}, Flour 240 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A recipe for making $16$ cupcakes includes the following ingredients:

Sugar
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {120} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 12} {12} \times 16 \\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {12} } {\cancel {12} } \times 16 \\\
&= 10 \times 16 \\\\
&= 160 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$ 

Eggs
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 4} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {3} } {\cancel {3} \times 4} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {4} } {\cancel {4} } \\\\
&= 4 
\end{aligned}
$ 

Butter
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {160} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 40} {3 \times 4} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 40} {3 \times \cancel {4} } \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {40 \times 16} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {640} {3} \\\\
&= 213.33 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$


Flour
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {180} {12} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 \times 6} {6 \times 2} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel{6} } {\cancel{6} \times 2} \times 16 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 \times 16} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {15 \times 2 \times 16} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {2}  \times 16} {\cancel {2} } \\\\
&= 240 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$Sugar 160 \ \text{g}, Eggs 4, Butter 213.33 \ \text{g}, Flour 240 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

Write in the missing digits to make this correct.

%waffles%

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's take the whole number (with missing digits) $=a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 7  &=  896 \\\\ 
a           &=  \dfrac {896} {7} \\\\
a           &=  \dfrac {128 \times 7} {7} \\\\
a           &=  \dfrac {128 \times \cancel{7}} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
a           &=  128
\end{aligned}
$

The missing digits are $1 \ \text{and} 8$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1 \ \text{and} 8$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

Sam thinks of a number. 
If you double the number and add $6$ you get the same answer as multiplying it by $3$ and subtracting $3$.

Find the number that Sam was thinking of. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a - 3  &= 2a + 6 \\\\
3a - 2a &= 6 + 3 \\\\
a       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a - 3  &= 2a + 6 \\\\
3a - 2a &= 6 + 3 \\\\
a       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A9712DDD-B6B1-40F8-80F2-D8628FB0991D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A square and an equilateral triangle are shown in the diagram.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Work out the size of the angle marked $x$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

According to question,

angle of each side of the square$=90\degree$

angle of each side of the equilateral triangle$=60\degree$

let the remaining angle  $=y$

$
\begin{aligned}
y + 80\degree = 180\degree \\\\
y = 180\degree - 80\degree \\\\
y= 100\degree
\end{aligned}
$

As we know sum of a quadrilateral $=360\degree$

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 60\degree + 100\degree +90\degree =360 \degree \\\\
x + 250\degree = 360\degree \\\\
x = 110\degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$110\degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>According to question,

angle of each side of the square$=90\degree$

angle of each side of the equilateral triangle$=60\degree$

let the remaining angle  $=y$

$
\begin{aligned}
y + 80\degree = 180\degree \\\\
y = 180\degree - 80\degree \\\\
y= 100\degree
\end{aligned}
$

As we know sum of a quadrilateral $=360\degree$

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 60\degree + 100\degree +90\degree =360 \degree \\\\
x + 250\degree = 360\degree \\\\
x = 110\degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$110\degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0015986A-8689-4697-907E-D8FF9E05B338'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Here are three squares that overlap.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


The non-overlapping parts of the squares have a total area of $90 \ \text{cm}^{2}$.

The areas of overlapping parts are $2 \ \text{cm}^{2}$, 
$3 \ \text{cm}^{2}$ and $4 \ \text{cm}^{2}$.

Work out the area of one of the squares. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

According to question,
 
area of non-overlapping squares $=90 cm^2$

area of non-overlapping of one squares $=90 cm^2 \div 3$

$ 90 \div{3} = 30cm^2$

area of overlapping parts $=2cm^2,3cm^2,4cm^2$

area of one square $= area of non-overlapping of one square + area of 2 overlapping parts$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 30cm^2 + 2cm^2 +3cm^2 \\\\
&=   35 cm^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 cm^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>According to question,
 
area of non-overlapping squares $=90 cm^2$

area of non-overlapping of one squares $=90 cm^2 \div 3$

$ 90 \div{3} = 30cm^2$

area of overlapping parts $=2cm^2,3cm^2,4cm^2$

area of one square $= area of non-overlapping of one square + area of 2 overlapping parts$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 30cm^2 + 2cm^2 +3cm^2 \\\\
&=   35 cm^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-CBEC2C7D-D491-48AE-96B9-A2E72A76F5A3'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


This diagram is made up of $7$ identical regular hexagons and $12$ identical equilateral triangles.

Note: the sides of the triangles are the same length as the sides of the hexagons.

Find the fraction of the diagram that is shaded. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {\text {Shaded portion} } {\text {Total portion} } \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {12 + 7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {19}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {7} {19}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {\text {Shaded portion} } {\text {Total portion} } \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {12 + 7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {19}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {7} {19}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-CB9E833C-477C-416F-9F7E-B57C7CF7ECEC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In Glasgow, the temperature is $-7 \degree \text{C}$ and in Exeter the temperature is $12$ degrees warmer. 

What is the temperature in Exeter? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {The temperature in Exeter}   &= 12 + (-7) \\\\
                                    &= 12 - 7 \\\\
                                    &= 5 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5 \degree \text {C}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {The temperature in Exeter}   &= 12 + (-7) \\\\
                                    &= 12 - 7 \\\\
                                    &= 5 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5 \degree \text {C}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C82E5C30-82DD-4D11-B4AF-0C2607D4E161'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What is the difference between $20 \%$ of $90$ and $90 \%$ of $20$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's calculate the first value.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 20 \% \ \text {of} \ 90 \\\\
&= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\
&= \dfrac {20 \times 90} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {20 \times 5 \times 18} {20 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5} \times 18} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Let's calculate the second value.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 90 \% \ \text {of} \ 20 \\\\
&= \dfrac {90} {100} \times 20 \\\\
&= \dfrac {90 \times 20} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {18 \times 5 \times 20} {5 \times 20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {20}} \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Using the calculation above,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \text {first value} - \text {second value} \\\\
&= 18 - 18 \\\\
&= 0
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's calculate the first value.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 20 \% \ \text {of} \ 90 \\\\
&= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\
&= \dfrac {20 \times 90} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {20 \times 5 \times 18} {20 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5} \times 18} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Let's calculate the second value.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 90 \% \ \text {of} \ 20 \\\\
&= \dfrac {90} {100} \times 20 \\\\
&= \dfrac {90 \times 20} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {18 \times 5 \times 20} {5 \times 20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {20}} \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Using the calculation above,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \text {first value} - \text {second value} \\\\
&= 18 - 18 \\\\
&= 0
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E8BEF82D-E970-4508-A623-9BC202200751'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Jamie left school at $3:55 \ \text{pm}$ and arrived home $55 \ \text{minutes}$ later.

At what time did Jamie get home? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Arrival time}             &= 3 {:} 55 \ \text {pm} + 55 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                 &= 4 {:} 50 \ \text {pm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 {:} 50 \ \text {pm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Arrival time}             &= 3 {:} 55 \ \text {pm} + 55 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                 &= 4 {:} 50 \ \text {pm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 {:} 50 \ \text {pm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A2AB9E52-5757-48BA-893F-644438CCA57D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Joey has the six number cards shown below

$
\huge {
\boxed {1} 
\quad \boxed {2} 
\quad \boxed {3} 
\quad \boxed {4}
\quad \boxed {5}
\quad \boxed {8}
}
$

The number cards are to be placed next to each other to form different numbers.

For example using three of the cards you could make two hundred and fifty one like this: 

$
\huge {
\boxed {2} 
\ \boxed {5} 
\ \boxed {1}
}
$


</div>

<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B260B37F-8BEE-47DF-AEC2-985D659409BC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Sujatha’s marks in $4$ tests were $16, 15, 16$ and $10$.

What was her mean score? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mean score}   &= \dfrac {\text {Total marks obtained}} {\text {Total number of tests}} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {16 + 15 + 16 + 10} {4} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {57} {4} \\\\
                     &= 14.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mean score}   &= \dfrac {\text {Total marks obtained}} {\text {Total number of tests}} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {16 + 15 + 16 + 10} {4} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {57} {4} \\\\
                     &= 14.25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4840C083-9CBF-43EF-9050-88F38D85B5EC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Tom goes to a theme park.

There are $4$ activities at the theme park.

The table gives information about these activities.
 
![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


Tom wants to do all $4$ activities.

He arrives at the theme park at $10 \ 20$ 

He needs to leave the theme park by $14 \ 30$.

Plan a schedule for Tom's visit to the theme park so he can do all $4$ activities.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
&   \text{Activity}   &   \text{Start time}   &   \text{ Finish  time} \\
&   \text{Penguin feeding}    &   10{:}45   &   11{:}20  \\
&   \text{Jeep safari}     &   12{:}00   &   12{:}45 \\
&   \text{Steam train ride}     &   14{:}00   &   14{:}25 \\
&   \text{Dolphin show}     &   13{:}00   &   13{:}40 
\end{array}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}

&   \text{Dolphin show}     &   13{:}00   &   13{:}40 
\end{array}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
y + 2y + 3y &= 180 \degree \\\\
6y          &= 180 \degree \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {180} {6} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {30 \times 6} {6} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel {6} } {\cancel {6} } \\\\
y           &= 30 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \degree$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

A cuboid has

a volume of $40 \ \text{cm}^{3}$

a length of $5 \ \text{cm}$

a width of $2 \ \text{cm}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the height of the cuboid.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let height of the cuboid $=a $

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Volume}                          &=  \text {length} \times \text {width} \times \text{height} \\\\
40                                      &=  5 \times 2 \times a \\\\
40                                      &=  10 \times a \\\\
\dfrac {40} {10}                        &=  a \\\\
\dfrac {4\cancel {0} } {1\cancel {0} }  &=  a \\\\
\text {a}                               &=  4 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Height of the cuboid $=  4 \ \text {cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let height of the cuboid $=a $

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Volume}                          &=  \text {length} \times \text {width} \times \text{height} \\\\
40                                      &=  5 \times 2 \times a \\\\
40                                      &=  10 \times a \\\\
\dfrac {40} {10}                        &=  a \\\\
\dfrac {4\cancel {0} } {1\cancel {0} }  &=  a \\\\
\text {a}                               &=  4 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Height of the cuboid $=  4 \ \text {cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


A carton measures $200 \ \text{cm}$ by $100 \ \text{cm}$ by $100 \ \text{cm}$.

The carton is to be completely filled with boxes.

Each box measures $50 \ \text{cm}$ by $20 \ \text{cm}$ by $20 \ \text{cm}$.

Work out the number of boxes which can completely fill the carton.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boxes}  &= \dfrac {\text {Volume of carton} } { \text {Volume of box} } \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {200 \times 100 \times 100} {50 \times 20 \times 20} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {20 \times 2 \times 5 \times 20 \times 50 \times 2} {50 \times 20 \times 20} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {\cancel{20} \times 2 \times 5 \times \cancel{20} \times \cancel{50} \times 2} {\cancel{50} \times \cancel{20} \times \cancel{20} } \\\\
                         &= 2 \times 5 \times 2 \\\\
                         &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boxes}  &= \dfrac {\text {Volume of carton} } { \text {Volume of box} } \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {200 \times 100 \times 100} {50 \times 20 \times 20} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {20 \times 2 \times 5 \times 20 \times 50 \times 2} {50 \times 20 \times 20} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {\cancel{20} \times 2 \times 5 \times \cancel{20} \times \cancel{50} \times 2} {\cancel{50} \times \cancel{20} \times \cancel{20} } \\\\
                         &= 2 \times 5 \times 2 \\\\
                         &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-19234F0C-4C85-4F00-8A0C-D18A9430B6A0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Four strips of paper are stuck on a table as shown. Each one is a rectangle that is $12 \ \text{cm}$ long and $2 \ \text{cm}$ wide.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


What area of the table is covered? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Since the rectangles are overlapping at four places, we need to subtract those area from the total.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of the table covered} &= \text {Total area of rectangles} - \text {overlapping area} \\\\
                                  &= (12 \times 2 \times 4) - (2 \times 2 \times 4) \\\\
                                  &= 96 - 16 \\\\
                                  &= 80 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$80 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

Since the rectangles are overlapping at four places, we need to subtract those area from the total.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of the table covered} &= \text {Total area of rectangles} - \text {overlapping area} \\\\
                                  &= (12 \times 2 \times 4) - (2 \times 2 \times 4) \\\\
                                  &= 96 - 16 \\\\
                                  &= 80 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$80 \ \text {cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-80391C1A-13B4-4C02-A048-8C0B0C4AF7B9'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A cuboid has faces with areas of $24 \ \text{cm}^{2}, 32 \ \text{cm}^{2}$ and $48 \ \text{cm}^{2}$.

What are the lengths of its edges? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the length of first edge of cuboid $= l$

Let the length of second edge of cuboid $= w$

Let the length of third edge cuboid $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
l \times w                                      &= 24 \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {l} \\\\
w \times h                                      &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24} {l} \times h                        &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24h} {l}                                &= 32 \\\\
24h                                             &= 32 \times l \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {32 \times l} {24} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
l \times \dfrac{32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{l \times 32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{32 \times l^2} {24}                      &= 48 \\\\
32 \times l^2                                   &= 48 \times 24 \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {48 \times 24} {32} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {16 \times 3 \times 2 \times 12} {16 \times 2} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 3 \times \cancel {2} \times 12} {\cancel {16} \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
l^2                                             &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
l^2                                             &= 36 \\\\
l                                               &= \sqrt {36} \\\\
l                                               &= 6 \ \text {cm} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 4} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
w                                               &= 4 \ \text {cm} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
6 \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {48} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 8} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 8} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
h                                               &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the edges of the cuboid are $6 \ \text {cm}, \ 4 \ \text {cm}, \ 8 \ \text {cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \ \text {cm}, \ 4 \ \text {cm}, \ 8 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the length of first edge of cuboid $= l$

Let the length of second edge of cuboid $= w$

Let the length of third edge cuboid $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
l \times w                                      &= 24 \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {l} \\\\
w \times h                                      &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24} {l} \times h                        &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24h} {l}                                &= 32 \\\\
24h                                             &= 32 \times l \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {32 \times l} {24} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
l \times \dfrac{32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{l \times 32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{32 \times l^2} {24}                      &= 48 \\\\
32 \times l^2                                   &= 48 \times 24 \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {48 \times 24} {32} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {16 \times 3 \times 2 \times 12} {16 \times 2} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 3 \times \cancel {2} \times 12} {\cancel {16} \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
l^2                                             &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
l^2                                             &= 36 \\\\
l                                               &= \sqrt {36} \\\\
l                                               &= 6 \ \text {cm} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 4} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
w                                               &= 4 \ \text {cm} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
6 \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {48} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 8} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 8} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
h                                               &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the edges of the cuboid are $6 \ \text {cm}, \ 4 \ \text {cm}, \ 8 \ \text {cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-83315ED9-DB65-48DC-A17E-D2511B4529A7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Three friends went to a burger restaurant.

Tom had a burger and chips and he paid $\pounds 8.00$.

Fred had two burgers and a drink. Fred paid $\pounds 15.00$.

Jo had a burger and a drink. Jo paid $\pounds 8.50$.

Work out the cost of the burger, the chips and the drink. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of a burger $= b$

Let the cost of chips $= c$

Let the cost of a drink $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Tom paid}    &= \pounds 8 \\\\
b + c               &= 8 \\\\
\text {Fred paid}   &= \pounds 15 \\\\
2b + d              &= 15 \\\\
\text {Jo paid}     &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
b + d               &= 8.50
\end{aligned}
$

Using the above two equations we get,

$
\begin{aligned} 
2b + d - b - d  &= 15 - 8.50 \\\\
b               &= \pounds 6.50
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of the burger $= \pounds 6.50$

$
\begin {aligned}
b + c       &= 8 \\\\
6.50 + c    &= 8 \\\\
c           &= 8 - 6.50 \\\\
c           &= \pounds 1.50
\end {aligned}
$

Cost of the chips $= \pounds 1.50$

$
\begin {aligned}
b + d       &= 8.50 \\\\
6.50 + d    &= 8.50 \\\\
d           &= 8.50 - 6.50 \\\\
d           &= \pounds 2
\end {aligned}
$

Cost of the drink $= \pounds 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Burger $=$ $\pounds 6.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Chips $=$ $\pounds 1.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Drink $=$ $\pounds 2$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

</div>
